========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Good to meet you too, Doc. Yeah, so... what made me come in is, well, for the past year or so, I just can't seem to keep my head in the game. The smallest thing pulls me off what I’m doing before I can even stop it. 

Like, my phone will buzz, or somebody will walk past my office. Honestly, putting in those floor-to-ceiling glass walls was the worst decision I ever made for that building. I told the architects I wanted a modern vibe, you know, since I built the company from the ground up and wanted it to look the part. But now every time someone goes to the breakroom, I see them. And they see me. It cost a fortune, too, the tempered glass is not cheap, let me tell you. 

But yeah, that, or I'll just be sitting there and get a random thought about my in-laws. Which, by the way, they're coming to stay with us next month, and my father-in-law is already complaining about the guest mattress. I bought a three-thousand-dollar mattress for that room! It's top of the line! How can you complain about... anyway. Wait, what was I saying? Right, yeah. Every little thing just pulls my attention away before I can catch myself.

High? No, I wouldn't say high. I mean, I don't feel like I'm buzzing out of my mind or anything like that. I've always had a lot of energy, sure—you don't build a company from scratch without a lot of hustle. But it's just my normal everyday energy.

My assistant, Sarah, she tried to use it the first day and completely blew a gasket, sprayed steam all over the quarterly projections. I mean, I dropped out of high school in the tenth grade and I can figure out a steam wand, but whatever.

Yeah, looking back, it definitely started when I was a kid. I mean, teachers were always moving my seat because I talked too much, or I'd just get up without asking, or I'd blurt things out before they even finished the question. 

Actually, I remember my fifth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive mole right on her chin, and it was so distracting. I mean, you couldn't look away from it. She used to make me sit right next to her desk, probably so she could keep an eye on me. But her desk was right by the window, which overlooked the playground. Who puts the problem kid next to the window? I used to watch the janitor, Mr. Henderson, mow the lawn. He had one of those old-school riding mowers, the ones that sputter and smell like cheap gasoline. I always wanted to drive one of those. Honestly, I should buy a riding mower for my property upstate. We have three acres, and the landscaping company charges a fortune. I could probably do it myself on the weekends. It’d be relaxing, right? Just me and the mower... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, I definitely had trouble paying attention back then, too.

Work problems? I mean, I’m still keeping up overall. The company is doing great, numbers are solid. But... yeah, I guess it is starting to cost me some patience from people at work. Like, when we have executive meetings, or these mentoring conversations I do with the junior guys. 

Take this kid, Jason. Smart guy, went to Wharton. But why do all these business school guys wear those puffy fleece vests indoors? It’s like a uniform for them. We have state-of-the-art climate control, Jason, it’s seventy-two degrees in the office, you don't need a vest! I should institute a dress code. Or maybe ban vests entirely. But anyway, I'm supposed to be mentoring him, and I'll just totally derail the conversation, and I can see him getting visibly annoyed when the meeting doesn't go the way it should. 

And it’s not just the office. It’s definitely costing me trust at home, too. Like when family plans get messed up because I get sidetracked. We were supposed to go to this charity dinner last weekend. Black tie. I actually own my tuxedo—custom fit, velvet lapels, really sharp. I refuse to rent, renting a suit is just throwing money away. But I got so distracted looking at boat listings online because I'm thinking of getting a pontoon for the lake house... they have these new ones with a built-in grill on the deck, which sounds amazing for the Fourth of July, right? 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, right, work problems. Yeah, so I'm keeping the business running fine, but people at work and at home are definitely getting frustrated with me when our plans and meetings just don't go how they're supposed to.

Recklessly? No, I wouldn't say I've ever had a period where I just went crazy with money. I mean, I spend money, sure. Like I mentioned with the espresso machine, and I'm looking at that pontoon boat. But I have the money to spend. It's not like I'm blowing my life savings or putting the company at risk. 

Speaking of the company's money, though, we just switched our corporate cards to this new rewards program. The points you get on travel are unbelievable. I fly a lot for work—mostly to the West Coast, checking in on our distribution centers out there. The flights are brutal, though. Six hours in the air, and even in first class, the food is terrible. Last time, they served this chicken dish that looked like it had been run over by a baggage cart. I ended up just eating the little packet of almonds they give you before takeoff. You know, I read somewhere that airlines buy those almonds by the ton, and they have whole warehouses just full of nuts. Imagine being the guy in charge of the nut warehouse. What a job. 

But no, I don't go on wild spending sprees or anything. I buy nice things, but it's consistent. Just normal spending for a guy in my position.

She used to make me sit right next to her desk, probably so she could keep an eye on me. But her desk was right by the window, which overlooked the playground. Who puts the problem kid next to the window? I used to watch the janitor, Mr. Henderson, mow the lawn. He had one of those old-school riding mowers, the ones that sputter and smell like cheap gasoline. I always wanted to drive one of those. Honestly, I should buy a riding mower for my property upstate. We have three acres, and the landscaping company charges a fortune. I could probably do it myself on the weekends. Went away? No, I wouldn't say it ever just... vanished. It’s always been humming in the background. If anything, it’s actually been noticeably harder to manage lately. For about a year and two months now, exactly. That’s when this whole massive conflict with my in-laws started, and it’s just been completely taking over our family life.

It's the best piece of kitchen tech I own. I actually bought the commercial-grade one for our house because the consumer models don't heat the water fast enough. I hate waiting for water to boil. It's like watching paint dry. 

Speaking of paint, we need to repaint the guest house. The contractor used this cheap matte finish, and it scuffs if you even look at it wrong. I specifically told him to use eggshell, but nobody listens to instructions anymore. It's impossible to find good contractors who actually pay attention to the details. I might just have my own maintenance guys from the company go over there and fix it. 

Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, if the attention stuff ever went away. Yeah, no, never went away. Like I said, it’s just been way worse this past year and a couple months with all the in-law drama stressing everybody out.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I guess they do. I definitely catch myself talking way more than I mean to. It happens a lot when I’m explaining a new plan at work. I'll get going on the strategy, and by the time I finally notice what's happening, the other person has barely gotten a single word in. 

Like just yesterday, I was laying out this new expansion strategy to my VP of Sales. We're looking at opening a facility in Texas. Austin, specifically. Everyone is moving to Austin right now. The tax incentives are incredible, though the real estate market is totally inflated. I was looking at commercial lots online, and the prices per square foot are just absurd compared to what they were even three years ago. I actually thought about buying a residential property out there just as a side investment. Maybe run it as an Airbnb? But managing short-term rentals is a complete nightmare. People just trash the places. My buddy owned a rental in Scottsdale and some bachelorette party booked it and broke a five-thousand-dollar chandelier. Which, honestly, who puts a five-thousand-dollar crystal chandelier in a rental property? That's just bad business. You gotta go with durable fixtures. Industrial chic, you know? Exposed brick, matte black metal piping... 

Wait, sorry, what did you ask me? Right, talking too much. Yeah, exactly like that. I'll be explaining the Texas plan to my VP, and I'll just keep rolling, and suddenly I realize he's just been staring at me for twenty minutes and hasn't spoken. So, yeah, that definitely happens.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Worrying? No, not really. I mean, I'm a CEO, so there's always something to handle, but I don't just sit around agonizing over things that aren't happening. I'm a problem solver. If there's an issue, I fix it and move on. 

Like with the supply chain delays we had last quarter. I didn't sit around worrying about it, I just flew out to the port in Long Beach and yelled at some people until our containers got moved. Have you ever been to a major shipping port? The scale of it is just mind-blowing. These massive cranes moving thousands of tons of steel like they're Lego blocks. I actually watched a documentary about the Panama Canal expansion the other night. They had to build these entirely new lock systems just to handle the newer, bigger cargo ships. It's an incredible engineering feat. The amount of concrete they poured... I think it was enough to build a highway from New York to Seattle or something crazy like that. 

Speaking of highways, the traffic on my commute has been horrendous lately. They've been doing construction on the overpass for six months and I swear I never see anyone actually working. Just a bunch of orange cones and guys standing around drinking coffee. I really should just start taking the helicopter from the regional airport, but the noise ordinance in our neighborhood makes it a hassle. The HOA president, Bob, is a real stickler for the rules. He actually tried to fine me for leaving my trash cans out two hours past the deadline. Can you believe that? Two hours. 

But no, I don't really worry about things unless there's an actual fire to put out. I'm too busy getting things done to just sit and stress over hypotheticals.

Just... regular me, just scattered.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, looking back, it definitely started when I was a kid. I mean, teachers were always moving my seat because I talked too much, or I'd just get up without asking, or I'd blurt things out before they even finished the question. \n\nActually, I remember my fifth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive mole right on her chin, and it was so distracting. I mean, you couldn't look away from it. She used to make me sit right next to her desk, probably so she could keep an eye on me. But her desk was right by the window, which overlooked the playground. Who puts the problem kid next to the window? I used to watch the janitor, Mr. Henderson, mow the lawn. He had one of those old-school riding mowers, the ones that sputter and smell like cheap gasoline. I always wanted to drive one of those. Honestly, I should buy a riding mower for my property upstate. We have three acres, and the landscaping company charges a fortune. I could probably do it myself on the weekends. It’d be relaxing, right? Just me and the mower... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, I definitely had trouble paying attention back then, too.\n\nDoctor: Has your distractibility been causing problems at work?\n\nPatient: Work problems? I mean, I’m still keeping up overall. The company is doing great, numbers are solid. But... yeah, I guess it is starting to cost me some patience from people at work. Like, when we have executive meetings, or these mentoring conversations I do with the junior guys. \n\nTake this kid, Jason. Smart guy, went to Wharton. But why do all these business school guys wear those puffy fleece vests indoors? It’s like a uniform for them. But anyway, I'm supposed to be mentoring him, and I'll just totally derail the conversation, and I can see him getting visibly annoyed when the meeting doesn't go the way it should. \n\nAnd it’s not just the office. Oh, right, work problems. Yeah, so I'm keeping the business running fine, but people at work and at home are definitely getting frustrated with me when our plans and meetings just don't go how they're supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you spent money much more recklessly than usual?\n\nPatient: Recklessly? No, I wouldn't say I've ever had a period where I just went crazy with money. I mean, I spend money, sure. Like I mentioned with the espresso machine, and I'm looking at that pontoon boat. But I have the money to spend. It's not like I'm blowing my life savings or putting the company at risk. \n\nSpeaking of the company's money, though, we just switched our corporate cards to this new rewards program. The points you get on travel are unbelievable. I fly a lot for work—mostly to the West Coast, checking in on our distribution centers out there. The flights are brutal, though. Six hours in the air, and even in first class, the food is terrible. Last time, they served this chicken dish that looked like it had been run over by a baggage cart. I ended up just eating the little packet of almonds they give you before takeoff. Start tasks and not finish them? No, not really. I mean, I get things done. If I start a project, I make sure it crosses the finish line. You kind of have to when you're running a company, right? You can't just leave things hanging.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Good to meet you too, Doc. Yeah, so... what made me come in is, well, for the past year or so, I just can't seem to keep my head in the game. The smallest thing pulls me off what I’m doing before I can even stop it. \n\nLike, my phone will buzz, or somebody will walk past my office. Honestly, putting in those floor-to-ceiling glass walls was the worst decision I ever made for that building. I told the architects I wanted a modern vibe, you know, since I built the company from the ground up and wanted it to look the part. But now every time someone goes to the breakroom, I see them. And they see me. It cost a fortune, too, the tempered glass is not cheap, let me tell you. \n\nBut yeah, that, or I'll just be sitting there and get a random thought about my in-laws. Which, by the way, they're coming to stay with us next month, and my father-in-law is already complaining about the guest mattress. I bought a three-thousand-dollar mattress for that room! It's top of the line! How can you complain about... anyway. Wait, what was I saying? Right, yeah. Every little thing just pulls my attention away before I can catch myself.\n\nDoctor: During these times when your attention is all over the place, do you also feel unusually energized or high?\n\nPatient: High? No, I wouldn't say high. I mean, I don't feel like I'm buzzing out of my mind or anything like that. I've always had a lot of energy, sure—you don't build a company from scratch without a lot of hustle. But it's just my normal everyday energy. \n\nThough, speaking of energy, I did just buy this ridiculous Italian espresso machine for the executive suite. It's got the dual boilers, the chrome finish... beautiful piece of machinery. My assistant, Sarah, she tried to use it the first day and completely blew a gasket, sprayed steam all over the quarterly projections. I had to send her down to the coffee shop on the corner just to pay their barista to teach her how to froth milk. Which is crazy, right? Paying someone to teach someone how to make coffee. I mean, I dropped out of high school in the tenth grade and I can figure out a steam wand, but whatever. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't feel unusually energized or anything. Just... regular me, just scattered.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, looking back, it definitely started when I was a kid. I mean, teachers were always moving my seat because I talked too much, or I'd just get up without asking, or I'd blurt things out before they even finished the question. \n\nActually, I remember my fifth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive mole right on her chin, and it was so distracting. I mean, you couldn't look away from it. She used to make me sit right next to her desk, probably so she could keep an eye on me. But her desk was right by the window, which overlooked the playground. Who puts the problem kid next to the window? I used to watch the janitor, Mr. Henderson, mow the lawn. He had one of those old-school riding mowers, the ones that sputter and smell like cheap gasoline. I always wanted to drive one of those. Honestly, I should buy a riding mower for my property upstate. We have three acres, and the landscaping company charges a fortune. I could probably do it myself on the weekends. It’d be relaxing, right? Just me and the mower... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, I definitely had trouble paying attention back then, too.\n\nDoctor: Has your distractibility been causing problems at work?\n\nPatient: Work problems? I mean, I’m still keeping up overall. The company is doing great, numbers are solid. But... yeah, I guess it is starting to cost me some patience from people at work. Like, when we have executive meetings, or these mentoring conversations I do with the junior guys. \n\nTake this kid, Jason. Smart guy, went to Wharton. But why do all these business school guys wear those puffy fleece vests indoors? It’s like a uniform for them. We have state-of-the-art climate control, Jason, it’s seventy-two degrees in the office, you don't need a vest! I should institute a dress code. Or maybe ban vests entirely. But anyway, I'm supposed to be mentoring him, and I'll just totally derail the conversation, and I can see him getting visibly annoyed when the meeting doesn't go the way it should. \n\nAnd it’s not just the office. It’s definitely costing me trust at home, too. Like when family plans get messed up because I get sidetracked. We were supposed to go to this charity dinner last weekend. Black tie. I actually own my tuxedo—custom fit, velvet lapels, really sharp. I refuse to rent, renting a suit is just throwing money away. But I got so distracted looking at boat listings online because I'm thinking of getting a pontoon for the lake house... they have these new ones with a built-in grill on the deck, which sounds amazing for the Fourth of July, right? \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right, work problems. Yeah, so I'm keeping the business running fine, but people at work and at home are definitely getting frustrated with me when our plans and meetings just don't go how they're supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you spent money much more recklessly than usual?\n\nPatient: Recklessly? No, I wouldn't say I've ever had a period where I just went crazy with money. I mean, I spend money, sure. Like I mentioned with the espresso machine, and I'm looking at that pontoon boat. But I have the money to spend. It's not like I'm blowing my life savings or putting the company at risk. \n\nSpeaking of the company's money, though, we just switched our corporate cards to this new rewards program. The points you get on travel are unbelievable. I fly a lot for work—mostly to the West Coast, checking in on our distribution centers out there. The flights are brutal, though. Six hours in the air, and even in first class, the food is terrible. Last time, they served this chicken dish that looked like it had been run over by a baggage cart. I ended up just eating the little packet of almonds they give you before takeoff. You know, I read somewhere that airlines buy those almonds by the ton, and they have whole warehouses just full of nuts. Imagine being the guy in charge of the nut warehouse. What a job. \n\nBut no, I don't go on wild spending sprees or anything. I buy nice things, but it's consistent. Just normal spending for a guy in my position.\n\nDoctor: Have there been stretches of time when your attention problems completely went away?\n\nPatient: Went away? No, I wouldn't say it ever just... vanished. It’s always been humming in the background. If anything, it’s actually been noticeably harder to manage lately. For about a year and two months now, exactly. That’s when this whole massive conflict with my in-laws started, and it’s just been completely taking over our family life. \n\nMy mother-in-law is... well, she's a lot. She insists on hosting these mandatory Sunday dinners, right? And she makes this incredibly dry, overcooked pot roast every single time. I actually offered to buy them a sous-vide machine—you know, just to help out. I have one, and it cooks meat perfectly. You just vacuum seal it, drop it in the water bath, and walk away. It's the best piece of kitchen tech I own. I actually bought the commercial-grade one for our house because the consumer models don't heat the water fast enough. I hate waiting for water to boil. It's like watching paint dry. \n\nSpeaking of paint, we need to repaint the guest house. The contractor used this cheap matte finish, and it scuffs if you even look at it wrong. I specifically told him to use eggshell, but nobody listens to instructions anymore. It's impossible to find good contractors who actually pay attention to the details. I might just have my own maintenance guys from the company go over there and fix it. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, if the attention stuff ever went away. Yeah, no, never went away. Like I said, it’s just been way worse this past year and a couple months with all the in-law drama stressing everybody out.\n\nDoctor: Do people often tell you that you talk too much?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess they do. I definitely catch myself talking way more than I mean to. It happens a lot when I’m explaining a new plan at work. I'll get going on the strategy, and by the time I finally notice what's happening, the other person has barely gotten a single word in. \n\nLike just yesterday, I was laying out this new expansion strategy to my VP of Sales. We're looking at opening a facility in Texas. Austin, specifically. Everyone is moving to Austin right now. The tax incentives are incredible, though the real estate market is totally inflated. I was looking at commercial lots online, and the prices per square foot are just absurd compared to what they were even three years ago. I actually thought about buying a residential property out there just as a side investment. Maybe run it as an Airbnb? But managing short-term rentals is a complete nightmare. People just trash the places. My buddy owned a rental in Scottsdale and some bachelorette party booked it and broke a five-thousand-dollar chandelier. Which, honestly, who puts a five-thousand-dollar crystal chandelier in a rental property? That's just bad business. You gotta go with durable fixtures. Industrial chic, you know? Exposed brick, matte black metal piping... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? Right, talking too much. Yeah, exactly like that. I'll be explaining the Texas plan to my VP, and I'll just keep rolling, and suddenly I realize he's just been staring at me for twenty minutes and hasn't spoken. So, yeah, that definitely happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying excessively, even when there isn’t an immediate problem to solve?\n\nPatient: Worrying? No, not really. I mean, I'm a CEO, so there's always something to handle, but I don't just sit around agonizing over things that aren't happening. I'm a problem solver. If there's an issue, I fix it and move on. \n\nLike with the supply chain delays we had last quarter. I didn't sit around worrying about it, I just flew out to the port in Long Beach and yelled at some people until our containers got moved. Have you ever been to a major shipping port? The scale of it is just mind-blowing. These massive cranes moving thousands of tons of steel like they're Lego blocks. I actually watched a documentary about the Panama Canal expansion the other night. They had to build these entirely new lock systems just to handle the newer, bigger cargo ships. It's an incredible engineering feat. The amount of concrete they poured... I think it was enough to build a highway from New York to Seattle or something crazy like that. \n\nSpeaking of highways, the traffic on my commute has been horrendous lately. They've been doing construction on the overpass for six months and I swear I never see anyone actually working. Just a bunch of orange cones and guys standing around drinking coffee. I really should just start taking the helicopter from the regional airport, but the noise ordinance in our neighborhood makes it a hassle. The HOA president, Bob, is a real stickler for the rules. He actually tried to fine me for leaving my trash cans out two hours past the deadline. Can you believe that? Two hours. \n\nBut no, I don't really worry about things unless there's an actual fire to put out. I'm too busy getting things done to just sit and stress over hypotheticals.\n\nDoctor: Do you often start tasks but fail to finish them because your mind drifts off?\n\nPatient: Start tasks and not finish them? No, not really. I mean, I get things done. If I start a project, I make sure it crosses the finish line. You kind of have to when you're running a company, right? You can't just leave things hanging. \n\nI mean, I delegate a lot, obviously. That's what good leadership is. You hire smart people and you let them execute. Like my new COO, Sarah. She's fantastic. I poached her from a rival firm—offered her a twenty percent bump and stock options. Honestly, stock options are the only way to retain top talent these days. The tech sector ruined compensation models for everyone else. Now every mid-level manager expects equity just for showing up. It's ridiculous. I remember when I started my first business—well, it was more of a hustle, really, selling refurbished electronics out of my garage back when I was a teenager. I didn't even finish high school, you know? Just dropped out and started hustling. \n\nMy dad was furious. He thought I was throwing my life away. He was an actuary—the most boring job on the planet. Just crunching mortality tables all day. No risk, no reward. He couldn't understand why I didn't want to just get a safe job with a pension. But who even has pensions anymore? It's all 401ks, and the market is so volatile right now. I was looking at my portfolio this morning, and the tech sector took a massive hit because of some new regulation in Europe. The EU is always passing these massive regulatory frameworks that just stifle innovation. The GDPR thing a few years ago? A complete nightmare for our marketing department. We had to scrub half our email lists.\n\nBut yeah, what were we talking about? Oh, finishing tasks. No, I definitely finish things. I might bounce around between a few different projects at once, but they all get done. I don't just leave things half-finished.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient who has come to a psychiatric clinic with Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder (Predominantly Hyperactive/Impulsive Presentation). ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I guess I do get restless. When I'm supposed to just sit still, it feels like there's a motor running inside me. Like during family dinners, or those church volunteer meetings my wife drags me to. I just have to move or do something with my hands. 

Those church meetings are brutal, honestly. They spend forty-five minutes debating whether to use paper or plastic tablecloths for the bake sale. It's like, just make a decision! I could have optimized their entire supply chain and boosted their fundraising margins by thirty percent in the time it takes them to pick a napkin color. I actually offered to run the bake sale last year, run it like a proper business, you know? Set up a tiered pricing model, maybe some premium bundled packages. But Pastor Dave was like, "It's about community, not profit." Which, fine, but community doesn't fix the hole in the sanctuary roof. 

I was looking at that roof last Sunday. The shingles are completely shot. They need a full tear-off and replacement. Probably looking at thirty grand, minimum. Construction costs are out of control right now. Lumber prices finally stabilized, but finding reliable contractors? Forget about it. I had a guy remodel my master bathroom, and he disappeared for three weeks right in the middle of the job. Just left all his tools in my bathtub. I almost threw them out on the lawn. 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, staying seated. Yeah, no, I definitely struggle with that. I'm always tapping my foot or messing with my phone or something. I just can't sit there doing nothing.

I actually watched a documentary about the Panama Canal expansion the other night. They had to build these entirely new lock systems just to handle the newer, bigger cargo ships. It's an incredible engineering feat. The amount of concrete they poured... I think it was enough to build a highway from New York to Seattle or something crazy like that. \n\nSpeaking of highways, the traffic on my commute has been horrendous lately. They've been doing construction on the overpass for six months and I swear I never see anyone actually working. Just a bunch of orange cones and guys standing around drinking coffee. He actually tried to fine me for leaving my trash cans out two hours past the deadline. Can you believe that? Two hours. \n\nBut no, I don't really worry about things unless there's an actual fire to put out. I'm too busy getting things done to just sit and stress over hypotheticals.\n\nDoctor: Do you often start tasks but fail to finish them because your mind drifts off?\n\nPatient: Start tasks and not finish them? No, not really. I mean, I get things done. If I start a project, I make sure it crosses the finish line. You kind of have to when you're running a company, right? It's all 401ks, and the market is so volatile right now. I was looking at my portfolio this morning, and the tech sector took a massive hit because of some new regulation in Europe. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.   "reasoning": {
    "S021": "The patient's speech demonstrates tangentiality by drifting into an unrelated story about an espresso machine and their assistant, and they self-describe as being 'scattered'.",
    "S033": "The patient explicitly denies feeling 'high' or 'buzzing out of my mind'.",
    "S034": "The patient explicitly denies feeling 'unusually energized', stating it is just their normal everyday energy."
  }

She used to make me sit right next to her desk, probably so she could keep an eye on me. But her desk was right by the window, which overlooked the playground. Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings). I actually own my tuxedo—custom fit, velvet lapels, really sharp. I refuse to rent, renting a suit is just throwing money away. But I got so distracted looking at boat listings online because I'm thinking of getting a pontoon for the lake house... they have these new ones with a built-in grill on the deck, which sounds amazing for the Fourth of July, right? \n\nWait, what was the question? Subtypes: Auditory Hallucinations, Visual Hallucinations, Tactile Hallucinations, Olfactory Hallucinations, Gustatory Hallucinations\n[S021] Disorganized_Thinking_Speech: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.  Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nRecklessly? No, I wouldn't say I've ever had a period where I just went crazy with money. I mean, I spend money, sure. Like I mentioned with the espresso machine, and I'm looking at that pontoon boat. But I have the money to spend. It's not like I'm blowing my life savings or putting the company at risk. \n\nSpeaking of the company's money, though, we just switched our corporate cards to this new rewards program. The points you get on travel are unbelievable. I fly a lot for work—mostly to the West Coast, checking in on our distribution centers out there. The flights are brutal, though. Six hours in the air, and even in first class, the food is terrible. Last time, they served this chicken dish that looked like it had been run over by a baggage cart. I ended up just eating the little packet of almonds they give you before takeoff. You know, I read somewhere that airlines buy those almonds by the ton, and they have whole warehouses just full of nuts. Imagine being the guy in charge of the nut warehouse. What a job. \n\nBut no, I don't go on wild spending sprees or anything. I buy nice things, but it's consistent. Just normal spending for a guy in my position.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"

I really should just start taking the helicopter from the regional airport, but the noise ordinance in our neighborhood makes it a hassle. The HOA president, Bob, is a real stickler for the rules. He actually tried to fine me for leaving my trash cans out two hours past the deadline. Can you believe that? Two hours. \n\nBut no, I don't really worry about things unless there's an actual fire to put out. I'm too busy getting things done to just sit and stress over hypotheticals.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nStart tasks and not finish them? No, not really. I mean, I get things done. If I start a project, I make sure it crosses the finish line. You kind of have to when you're running a company, right? You can't just leave things hanging. \n\nI mean, I delegate a lot, obviously. That's what good leadership is. You hire smart people and you let them execute. Like my new COO, Sarah. She's fantastic. I poached her from a rival firm—offered her a twenty percent bump and stock options. Honestly, stock options are the only way to retain top talent these days. The tech sector ruined compensation models for everyone else. Now every mid-level manager expects equity just for showing up. It's ridiculous. I remember when I started my first business—well, it was more of a hustle, really, selling refurbished electronics out of my garage back when I was a teenager. I didn't even finish high school, you know? Just dropped out and started hustling. \n\nMy dad was furious. He thought I was throwing my life away. He was an actuary—the most boring job on the planet. Just crunching mortality tables all day. No risk, no reward. He couldn't understand why I didn't want to just get a safe job with a pension. But who even has pensions anymore? It's all 401ks, and the market is so volatile right now. I was looking at my portfolio this morning, and the tech sector took a massive hit because of some new regulation in Europe. The EU is always passing these massive regulatory frameworks that just stifle innovation. The GDPR thing a few years ago? A complete nightmare for our marketing department. We had to scrub half our email lists.\n\nBut yeah, what were we talking about? Oh, finishing tasks. No, I definitely finish things. I might bounce around between a few different projects at once, but they all get done. I don't just leave things half-finished.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"
  }

Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Just left all his tools in my bathtub. I almost threw them out on the lawn. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, staying seated. Yeah, no, I definitely struggle with that. I'm always tapping my foot or messing with my phone or something.
